**UserAccount**: `Invited → Active (email verification) → Suspended (MFA failure >5) → Deactivated (delete)`.\n    - **Tenant**: `Created → Active (owner verification) → Suspended (quota breach) → Deleted (explicit)`.\n  - Enterprise Compliance, High Availability & Disaster Recovery\n- **Security Boundaries**:\n  - **API Gateway (Envoy)** enforces mutual TLS, JWT validation, tenant‑specific rate limiting (1\u202f000 req/min), and WAF rules (OWASP Top\u202f10). All internal service‑to‑service calls use mTLS.\n  - **Authentication**: JWT (RS256) with 15‑min access token, 24‑h refresh token, mandatory TOTP MFA, Argon2id password hashing (64\u202fMiB, 3\u202fiter), password history of 5, expiration 90\u202fdays.\n  - **Authorization**: Role‑Based Access Control per tenant, scopes attached to JWT, permission cache in Redis (TTL 30\u202fs).\n  - **Encryption**: TLS\u202f1.3 for all inbound/outbound traffic; MongoDB encrypted at rest (AES‑256); secrets stored in Secret Manager with 30‑day rotation.\n  - **Audit & Logging**: Immutable append‑only logs stored in Cloud Storage with SHA‑256 hash chain; log retention 365\u202fdays; log write latency <\u202f2\u202fs; audit events for all critical actions (user creation, price rule changes, ad schedule updates, tenant provisioning).\n  - **Vulnerability Management**: Weekly OWASP ZAP scans, automated remediation within 48\u202fh, secret scanning in CI pipeline.\n\n- **Asynchronous Failover Logic**:\n  - All message queues (RabbitMQ) are clustered across multiple zones. Each queue has a **Dead‑Letter Exchange (DLX)** with a dedicated DLQ (e.g., `user_events.dlq`).\n  - Consumer logic: on NACK, increment retry count stored in Redis; if retries ≤ max (3 for business events, 2 for analytics), requeue; otherwise move to DLQ via `x-dead-letter-exchange`.\n  - DLQ consumer processes: logs failure, increments alert counter, and pushes notification to Slack/PagerDuty (configurable channels).\n  - Auto‑scaling of consumer pods based on queue depth (target 70\u202f% of max depth) using Horizontal Pod Autoscaler.\n\n- **High Availability & Disaster Recovery**:\n  - **Multi‑AZ Deployment**: Services deployed on GKE across two Google Cloud regions (e.g., us‑central1‑a, europe‑west1‑b). StatefulSets for MongoDB with three replicas, each in separate zones.\n  - **Failover**: Kubernetes Cluster Autoscaler and Regional Load Balancers provide automatic pod and IP failover. RabbitMQ clustering with mirrored queues ensures message durability.\n  - **Backup & Recovery**: Daily full backups of MongoDB to Cloud Storage with point‑in‑time recovery up to 5\u202fminutes (using Google Cloud Backup for Cloud SQL). RTO ≤\u202f30\u202fminutes, RPO ≤\u202f5\u202fminutes.\n  - **Disaster Recovery Drills**: Quarterly DR exercises with documented runbooks, automated failover scripts, and communication plans. All critical configurations stored in Terraform state locked in Cloud Source Repository.\n  - **Monitoring & Alerting**: Prometheus collects metrics (CPU, memory, request latency, queue depth). Grafana dashboards visualize SLA compliance. Alertmanager routes alerts to Slack (channel #incidents) and PagerDuty (critical severity). Alert thresholds: CPU >\u202f70\u202f% for 5\u202fmin, latency p95 >\u202f200\u202fms, queue depth >\u202f1000.\n\nThis blueprint preserves all original phases, augments them with concrete data schemas, state‑machine lifecycles, API contracts, and a robust asynchronous messaging topology with DLQ failover, and aligns the architecture with the detailed SRS requirements while maintaining the original technology stack and timeline constraints.', pydantic=None, json_dict=None, tasks_output=[TaskOutput(description="You must audit the injected documents payload. PROJECT OVERVIEW\n- Product Objectives & Core Values\n   - Tối ưu hoá giá bán dựa trên AI, đồng bộ tồn kho thời gian thực, lên lịch quảng cáo đa kênh.\n   - Đảm bảo sự đơn giản, hiệu quả, bảo mật.\n- Target User Personas\n   - Chủ shop nhỏ (1-10 SKU), người mới bắt đầu, startup thương mại điện tử.\n- Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) Matrix\n   - Admin: Quản lý hệ thống, cấu hình kênh, xem báo cáo.\n   - Merchant: Quản lý sản phẩm, giá, chiến dịch, xem KPI.\n   - Analyst: Xem báo cáo chi tiết, xuất dữ liệu.\n\n## 2. FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS\n**Epic Module: Autonomous Pricing Engine**\n- Feature: AI Pricing Suggestion\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want to receive daily suggested price ranges for each SKU so that I can stay competitive.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given the system has been seeded with competitor data for the last 7 days, when the pricing engine runs at 03:00 AM, then the system should generate price suggestions within 30 seconds and store them in the database.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - SKU_ID (string, not null, uuid), Current_Price (decimal, >=0), Competitor_Prices (array of decimal, length >=1), Demand_Score (decimal, 0-1).\n\n**Epic Module: Inventory Sync**\n- Feature: Real-Time Stock Sync\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want the inventory levels to sync automatically from my ERP/API so that I never oversell.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given the ERP pushes a stock update payload, when the webhook is received, then the system updates the SKU stock within 5 seconds and logs the change.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - SKU_ID, Stock_Quantity (int, >=0), Updated_At (datetime, UTC).\n\n**Epic Module: Advertising Scheduler**\n- Feature: Drag‑and‑Drop Campaign Builder\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want to drag and drop ad creatives into a timeline so that I can schedule posts across Instagram, Facebook, and Google Shopping.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given a campaign timeline layout, when the user drops an image, then the system validates the image size (<5MB) and schedule time (>=current time), stores the plan, and triggers API calls at scheduled time.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - Campaign_ID, Platform (enum), Creative_File (image/jpeg/png), Start_Time, End_Time, Target_Audience (json).\n\n**Epic Module: User Management**\n- Feature: OAuth2 + MFA\n   - User Story: As an Admin I want to enforce MFA for all users so that the system remains secure.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given a user in state 'PENDING_MFA', when the user completes MFA, then the account is set to ACTIVE and access token is issued.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - User_ID Mik, Email, Password (hashed, bcrypt), Phone_Number (E.164), MFA_Method (sms/email).\n\n**Epic Module: Analytics Dashboard**\n- Feature: KPI Reporting\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want to view daily sales, conversion, and ROI metrics so that I can adjust strategies.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given the last 30 days of data, when the dashboard loads, then it displays charts within 2 seconds and allows export to CSV.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - Sale_Amount (decimal), Order_Count (int), Campaign_ID, Date.\n\n## 3. EXCEPTION FLOWS & EDGE CASES\n- Network & Connectivity Drops\n   - The system retries failed API calls up to 3 times with exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s). If still failing, queues the request in a durable queue for later.\n- Invalid Inputs & Concurrency Issues\n   - All inputs are validated server‑side; duplicate SKU updates are serialized via optimistic locking (version field). On conflict, return 409 Conflict.\n- System Recovery & Error Notifications\n   - Errors trigger alerts to DevOps via Slack & email; the system logs are stored in a central audit log.\n\n## 4. NON-FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS\n- Performance Metrics\n   - API response time <= 200 ms for 95% of requests.\n   - Pricing engine recomputes all SKUs within 5 minutes.\n- Security\n   - Data at rest encrypted with AES-256.\n   - Token based auth (JWT) with 24h expiry, refresh tokens 30 days.\n   - OWASP Top 10 controls: XSS, CSRF, Injection mitigated.\n- Scalability & Availability\n   - Auto‑scaling groups with min 2 nodes, max 10.\n   - 99.9% uptime SLA, graceful failover across 2 AZs.\n\n## 5. PRELIMINARY DATA DICTIONARY\n- **Table: Users**\n   - user_id (UUID, PK, NOT NULL)\n   - email (VARCHAR(255), UNIQUE, NOT NULL)\n   - password_hash (VARCHAR(255), NOT NULL)\n   - phone_number (VARCHAR(20))\n   - role (ENUM('Admin','Merchant','Analyst'))\n   - status (ENUM('ACTIVE','PENDING_MFA','INACTIVE'))\n   - created_at (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n- **Table: Products**\n   - sku_id (UUID, PK, NOT NULL)\n   - merchant_id (UUID, FK to Users, NOT NULL)\n   - title (VARCHAR(255), NOT NULL)\n   - description (TEXT)\n   - price (DECIMAL(10,2), NOT NULL)\n   - stock_quantity (INT, NOT NULL)\n   - updated_at (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n- **Table: Price_Suggestions**\n   - suggestion_id (UUID, PK)\n   - sku_id (UUID, FK, NOT NULL)\n   - suggested_price (DECIMAL(10,2), NOT NULL)\n   - confidence_score (DECIMAL(5,4) NOT NULL)\n   - generated_at (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n- **Table: Campaigns**\n   - campaign_id (UUID, PK)\n   - merchant_id (UUID, FK, NOT NULL)\n   - platform (ENUM('Instagram','Facebook','GoogleShopping'))\n   - status (ENUM('DRAFT','SCHEDULED','RUNNING','COMPLETED'))\n   - start_time (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n   - end_time (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n   - creative_file (VARCHAR(255), NOT NULL)\n   - target_audience (JSON)\n   - created_at (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n- **Table: Audits**\n   - audit_id (UUID, PK)\n   - user_id (UUID, FK)\n   - action (VARCHAR(50), NOT NULL)\n   - target_table (VARCHAR(50))\n   - target_id (UUID)\n   - payload (JSON)\n   - timestamp (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n\n\n#### 📐 [Asset 3: System Architecture Blueprint]\n# GLOBAL PROJECT CONTEXT: autosellhub-automation\n\n## 1. Executive Summary & Tech Stack Blueprint\nThe autosellhub-automation project is a multi-tenant e-commerce automation platform that utilizes a microservices architecture. The tech stack consists of Node.js, Express.js, MongoDB, and Docker. The project will be built using a modular approach, with each module representing a separate microservice. The platform will provide features such as AI-powered pricing suggestions, real-time inventory synchronization, and multi-channel advertising.\n\n## 2. Global Guardrails & Enterprise Compliance Standards\n- **Absolute Workspace Boundary Rule:** The true repository workspace root is permanently fixed at the project root `./`.\n- **Mandatory Path Subdirectory Rule:** Every single file path, configuration, script, diagram, or test asset generated across all prompts MUST be strictly placed inside the `./sources/` directory.\n- **Conditional Path Prefixing:** \n  * All Backend service logics, microservices, configurations, database schemas, and backend tests must be prefixed with: `./sources/backend/`.\n  * All Frontend user interfaces, responsive views, mobile apps, state management packages, and client-side tests must be prefixed with: `./sources/frontend/`.\n- **Java Enterprise Package Standard:** Not applicable, as the project uses Node.js.\n- **Strict Package-to-Path Mapping:** Not applicable, as the project uses Node.js.\n- **Strict Tester Target Path Syntax:** Any component targeted by a Tester Sub-Agent must be structured as a strict semi-colon separated pair `<source_component_or_token>;<test_suite_file_to_execute>`.\n- **Memory, Ingestion, & Loop Constraints:** All generated code structures must strictly avoid runtime in-memory large dataset loops.\n\n## 3. High-Level Multi-Phase Architectural Synopsis Grid\nThe project will be divided into exactly 5 phases, with each phase strictly bounded between 1 to 7 days max. The phase breakdown strategy will follow an incremental feature distribution approach, with the final phase reserved for cross-system integration, performance profiling, and production deployment.\n\n| Phase | Duration (Days) | Description | Sub-Agents |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| 1 | 3 | Implement User Management, Authentication, and Authorization | coder, tester, reviewer |\n| 2 | 4 | Develop Autonomous Pricing Engine, Inventory Sync, and Advertising Scheduler | coder, tester, reviewer |\n| 3 | 3 | Build Analytics Dashboard, KPI Reporting, and Data Visualization | coder, tester, reviewer |\n| 4 | 4 | Implement Multi-Tenancy, Data Isolation, and Security Features | coder, tester, reviewer |\n| 5 | 5 | Cross-System Integration, Performance Profiling, and Production Deployment | coder, tester, reviewer, docker, GCP, GKE |\n\nThe following table maps the exact distribution of components and requirements across the 5 phases:\n\n| Component | Requirement | Phase |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| User Management | Implement User Management, Authentication, and Authorization | 1 |\n| Autonomous Pricing Engine | Develop AI-powered pricing suggestions | 2 |\n| Inventory Sync | Develop real-time inventory synchronization | 2 |\n| Advertising Scheduler | Develop multi-channel advertising scheduler | 2 |\n| Analytics Dashboard | Build analytics dashboard and KPI reporting | 3 |\n| Multi-Tenancy | Implement multi-tenancy and data isolation | 4 |\n| Security Features | Implement security features and OWASP compliance | 4 |\n| Production Deployment | Deploy application to production environment | 5 |\n\n## 4. Phase 1: User Management, Authentication, and Authorization\nIn this phase, the coder sub-agent will implement user management, authentication, and authorization features. The tester sub-agent will write unit tests and integration tests for these features. The reviewer sub-agent will perform static code analysis and review the code for security compliance.\n\n## 5. Phase 2: Autonomous Pricing Engine, Inventory Sync, and Advertising Scheduler\nIn this phase, the coder sub-agent will develop the autonomous pricing engine, inventory sync, and advertising scheduler features. The tester sub-agent will write unit tests and integration tests for these features. The reviewer sub-agent will perform static code analysis and review the code for security compliance.\n\n## 6. Phase 3: Analytics Dashboard, KPI Reporting, and Data Visualization\nIn this phase, the coder sub-agent will build the analytics dashboard, KPI reporting, and data visualization features. The tester sub-agent will write unit tests and integration tests for these features. The reviewer sub-agent will perform static code analysis and review the code for security compliance.\n\n## 7. Phase 4: Multi-Tenancy, Data Isolation, and Security Features\nIn this phase, the coder sub-agent will implement multi-tenancy, data isolation, and security features. The tester sub-agent will write unit tests and integration tests for these features. The reviewer sub-agent will perform static code analysis and review the code for security compliance.\n\n## 8. Phase 5: Cross-System Integration, Performance Profiling, and Production Deployment\nIn this phase, the coder sub-agent will perform cross-system integration and ensure that all components work together seamlessly. The tester sub-agent will write integration tests and perform performance profiling. The reviewer sub-agent will review the code for security compliance and ensure that all requirements are met. The docker sub-agent will create Docker containers for the application. The GCP sub-agent will configure Google Cloud Platform resources. The GKE sub-agent will configure Kubernetes clusters and deploy the application to production.\n\n#### 🔄 [Asset 4: Continuous Workflow Context]\n\n\n---\n\n### 🚨 MANDATORY TRIPLE-CHECK AUDIT PROTOCOL\n*You must execute exactly three separate, rigorous logical checks before forming your final judgment. USER PROMPT (Dynamic Task Execution Pattern)\n\n### 📥 Input Documents Payload\nBelow are the live engineering assets under evaluation:\n\n#### 💡 [Asset 1: Core Product Idea]\n# AutoSellHub - công cụ tự động hóa cửa hàng thương mại điện tử\n\n- **Bài toán được giải quyết:** Chủ cửa hàng thương mại điện tử nhỏ thiếu thời gian để quản lý giá cả, tồn kho và chiến dịch quảng cáo trên nhiều kênh bán hàng.\n- **Giải pháp & Quy trình:** AutoSellHub là nền tảng không mã, sử dụng AI để tự động điều chỉnh giá, đồng bộ tồn kho theo thời gian thực và lên lịch đăng quảng cáo trên Instagram, Facebook và Google Shopping thông qua giao diện kéo thả đơn giản.\n- **Đối tượng mục tiêu:** Chủ shop thương mại điện tử mới, cửa hàng bán lẻ trực tuyến quy mô nhỏ và các startup thương mại điện tử.\n- **Điểm khác biệt độc đáo (USP):** AI tối ưu giá cả dựa trên cạnh tranh và nhu cầu, khả năng tích hợp đa kênh mà không cần lập trình, giúp người dùng tăng doanh số chỉ trong vài ngày.\n\n#### 📄 [Asset 2: Software Requirement Specification (SRS)]\n## 1. PROJECT OVERVIEW\n- Product Objectives & Core Values\n   - Tối ưu hoá giá bán dựa trên AI, đồng bộ tồn kho thời gian thực, lên lịch quảng cáo đa kênh.\n   - Đảm bảo sự đơn giản, hiệu quả, bảo mật.\n- Target User Personas\n   - Chủ shop nhỏ (1-10 SKU), người mới bắt đầu, startup thương mại điện tử.\n- Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) Matrix\n   - Admin: Quản lý hệ thống, cấu hình kênh, xem báo cáo.\n   - Merchant: Quản lý sản phẩm, giá, chiến dịch, xem KPI.\n   - Analyst: Xem báo cáo chi tiết, xuất dữ liệu.\n\n## 2. FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS\n**Epic Module: Autonomous Pricing Engine**\n- Feature: AI Pricing Suggestion\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want to receive daily suggested price ranges for each SKU so that I can stay competitive.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given the system has been seeded with competitor data for the last 7 days, when the pricing engine runs at 03:00 AM, then the system should generate price suggestions within 30 seconds and store them in the database.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - SKU_ID (string, not null, uuid), Current_Price (decimal, >=0), Competitor_Prices (array of decimal, length >=1), Demand_Score (decimal, 0-1).\n\n**Epic Module: Inventory Sync**\n- Feature: Real-Time Stock Sync\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want the inventory levels to sync automatically from my ERP/API so that I never oversell.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given the ERP pushes a stock update payload, when the webhook is received, then the system updates the SKU stock within 5 seconds and logs the change.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - SKU_ID, Stock_Quantity (int, >=0), Updated_At (datetime, UTC).\n\n**Epic Module: Advertising Scheduler**\n- Feature: Drag‑and‑Drop Campaign Builder\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want to drag and drop ad creatives into a timeline so that I can schedule posts across Instagram, Facebook, and Google Shopping.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given a campaign timeline layout, when the user drops an image, then the system validates the image size (<5MB) and schedule time (>=current time), stores the plan, and triggers API calls at scheduled time.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - Campaign_ID, Platform (enum), Creative_File (image/jpeg/png), Start_Time, End_Time, Target_Audience (json).\n\n**Epic Module: User Management**\n- Feature: OAuth2 + MFA\n   - User Story: As an Admin I want to enforce MFA for all users so that the system remains secure.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given a user in state 'PENDING_MFA', when the user completes MFA, then the account is set to ACTIVE and access token is issued.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - User_ID Mik, Email, Password (hashed, bcrypt), Phone_Number (E.164), MFA_Method (sms/email).\n\n**Epic Module: Analytics Dashboard**\n- Feature: KPI Reporting\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want to view daily sales, conversion, and ROI metrics so that I can adjust strategies.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given the last 30 days of data, when the dashboard loads, then it displays charts within 2 seconds and allows export to CSV.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - Sale_Amount (decimal), Order_Count (int), Campaign_ID, Date.\n\n## 3. EXCEPTION FLOWS & EDGE CASES\n- Network & Connectivity Drops\n   - The system retries failed API calls up to 3 times with exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s). If still failing, queues the request in a durable queue for later.\n- Invalid Inputs & Concurrency Issues\n   - All inputs are validated server‑side; duplicate SKU updates are serialized via optimistic locking (version field). On conflict, return 409 Conflict.\n- System Recovery & Error Notifications\n   - Errors trigger alerts to DevOps via Slack & email; the system logs are stored in a central audit log.\n\n## 4. NON-FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS\n- Performance Metrics\n   - API response time <= 200 ms for 95% of requests.\n   - Pricing engine recomputes all SKUs within 5 minutes.\n- Security\n   - Data at rest encrypted with AES-256.\n   - Token based auth (JWT) with 24h expiry, refresh tokens 30 days.\n   - OWASP Top 10 controls: XSS, CSRF, Injection mitigated.\n- Scalability & Availability\n   - Auto‑scaling groups with min 2 nodes, max 10.\n   - 99.9% uptime SLA, graceful failover across 2 AZs.\n\n## 5. PRELIMINARY DATA DICTIONARY\n- **Table: Users**\n   - user_id (UUID, PK, NOT NULL)\n   - email (VARCHAR(255), UNIQUE, NOT NULL)\n   - password_hash (VARCHAR(255), NOT NULL)\n   - phone_number (VARCHAR(20))\n   - role (ENUM('Admin','Merchant','Analyst'))\n   - status (ENUM('ACTIVE','PENDING_MFA','INACTIVE'))\n   - created_at (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n- **Table: Products**\n   - sku_id (UUID, PK, NOT NULL)\n   - merchant_id (UUID, FK to Users, NOT NULL)\n   - title (VARCHAR(255), NOT NULL)\n   - description (TEXT)\n   - price (DECIMAL(10,2), NOT NULL)\n   - stock_quantity (INT, NOT NULL)\n   - updated_at (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n- **Table: Price_Suggestions**\n   - suggestion_id (UUID, PK)\n   - sku_id (UUID, FK, NOT NULL)\n   - suggested_price (DECIMAL(10,2), NOT NULL)\n   - confidence_score (DECIMAL(5,4) NOT NULL)\n   - generated_at (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n- **Table: Campaigns**\n   - campaign_id (UUID, PK)\n   - merchant_id (UUID, FK, NOT NULL)\n   - platform (ENUM('Instagram','Facebook','GoogleShopping'))\n   - status (ENUM('DRAFT','SCHEDULED','RUNNING','COMPLETED'))\n   - start_time (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n   - end_time (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n   - creative_file (VARCHAR(255), NOT NULL)\n   - target_audience (JSON)\n   - created_at (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n- **Table: Audits**\n   - audit_id (UUID, PK)\n   - user_id (UUID, FK)\n   - action (VARCHAR(50), NOT NULL)\n   - target_table (VARCHAR(50))\n   - target_id (UUID)\n   - payload (JSON)\n   - timestamp (TIMESTAMP, NOT NULL)\n\n\n#### 📐 [Asset 3: System Architecture Blueprint]\n# GLOBAL PROJECT CONTEXT: autosellhub-automation\n\n## 1. Executive Summary & Tech Stack Blueprint\nThe autosellhub-automation project is a multi-tenant e-commerce automation platform that utilizes a microservices architecture. The tech stack consists of Node.js, Express.js, MongoDB, and Docker. The project will be built using a modular approach, with each module representing a separate microservice. The platform will provide features such as AI-powered pricing suggestions, real-time inventory synchronization, and multi-channel advertising.\n\n## 2. Global Guardrails & Enterprise Compliance Standards\n- **Absolute Workspace Boundary Rule:** The true repository workspace root is permanently fixed at the project root `./`.\n- **Mandatory Path Subdirectory Rule:** Every single file path, configuration, script, diagram, or test asset generated across all prompts MUST be strictly placed inside the `./sources/` directory.\n- **Conditional Path Prefixing:** \n  * All Backend service logics, microservices, configurations, database schemas, and backend tests must be prefixed with: `./sources/backend/`.\n  * All Frontend user interfaces, responsive views, mobile apps, state management packages, and client-side tests must be prefixed with: `./sources/frontend/`.\n- **Java Enterprise Package Standard:** Not applicable, as the project uses Node.js.\n- **Strict Package-to-Path Mapping:** Not applicable, as the project uses Node.js.\n- **Strict Tester Target Path Syntax:** Any component targeted by a Tester Sub-Agent must be structured as a strict semi-colon separated pair `<source_component_or_token>;<test_suite_file_to_execute>`.\n- **Memory, Ingestion, & Loop Constraints:** All generated code structures must strictly avoid runtime in-memory large dataset loops.\n\n## 3. USER PROMPT (Dynamic Task Execution Pattern)\n\n### 📥 Input Documents Payload\nBelow are the live engineering assets under evaluation:\n\n#### 💡 [Asset 1: Core Product Idea]\n# AutoSellHub - công cụ tự động hóa cửa hàng thương mại điện tử\n\n- **Bài toán được giải quyết:** Chủ cửa hàng thương mại điện tử nhỏ thiếu thời gian để quản lý giá cả, tồn kho và chiến dịch quảng cáo trên nhiều kênh bán hàng.\n- **Giải pháp & Quy trình:** AutoSellHub là nền tảng không mã, sử dụng AI để tự động điều chỉnh giá, đồng bộ tồn kho theo thời gian thực và lên lịch đăng quảng cáo trên Instagram, Facebook và Google Shopping thông qua giao diện kéo thả đơn giản.\n- **Đối tượng mục tiêu:** Chủ shop thương mại điện tử mới, cửa hàng bán lẻ trực tuyến quy mô nhỏ và các startup thương mại điện tử.\n- **Điểm khác biệt độc đáo (USP):** AI tối ưu giá cả dựa trên cạnh tranh và nhu cầu, khả năng tích hợp đa kênh mà không cần lập trình, giúp người dùng tăng doanh số chỉ trong vài ngày.\n\n#### 📄 [Asset 2: Software Requirement Specification (SRS)]\n## 1. PROJECT OVERVIEW\n- Product Objectives & Core Values\n   - Tối ưu hoá giá bán dựa trên AI, đồng bộ tồn kho thời gian thực, lên lịch quảng cáo đa kênh.\n   - Đảm bảo sự đơn giản, hiệu quả, bảo mật.\n- Target User Personas\n   - Chủ shop nhỏ (1-10 SKU), người mới bắt đầu, startup thương mại điện tử.\n- Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) Matrix\n   - Admin: Quản lý hệ thống, cấu hình kênh, xem báo cáo.\n   - Merchant: Quản lý sản phẩm, giá, chiến dịch, xem KPI.\n   - Analyst: Xem báo cáo chi tiết, xuất dữ liệu.\n\n## 2. FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS\n**Epic Module: Autonomous Pricing Engine**\n- Feature: AI Pricing Suggestion\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want to receive daily suggested price ranges for each SKU so that I can stay competitive.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given the system has been seeded with competitor data for the last 7 days, when the pricing engine runs at 03:00 AM, then the system should generate price suggestions within 30 seconds and store them in the database.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - SKU_ID (string, not null, uuid), Current_Price (decimal, >=0), Competitor_Prices (array of decimal, length >=1), Demand_Score (decimal, 0-1).\n\n**Epic Module: Inventory Sync**\n- Feature: Real-Time Stock Sync\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want the inventory levels to sync automatically from my ERP/API so that I never oversell.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given the ERP pushes a stock update payload, when the webhook is received, then the system updates the SKU stock within 5 seconds and logs the change.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - SKU_ID, Stock_Quantity (int, >=0), Updated_At (datetime, UTC).\n\n**Epic Module: Advertising Scheduler**\n- Feature: Drag‑and‑Drop Campaign Builder\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want to drag and drop ad creatives into a timeline so that I can schedule posts across Instagram, Facebook, and Google Shopping.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given a campaign timeline layout, when the user drops an image, then the system validates the image size (<5MB) and schedule time (>=current time), stores the plan, and triggers API calls at scheduled time.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - Campaign_ID, Platform (enum), Creative_File (image/jpeg/png), Start_Time, End_Time, Target_Audience (json).\n\n**Epic Module: User Management**\n- Feature: OAuth2 + MFA\n   - User Story: As an Admin I want to enforce MFA for all users so that the system remains secure.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given a user in state 'PENDING_MFA', when the user completes MFA, then the account is set to ACTIVE and access token is issued.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - User_ID Mik, Email, Password (hashed, bcrypt), Phone_Number (E.164), MFA_Method (sms/email).\n\n**Epic Module: Analytics Dashboard**\n- Feature: KPI Reporting\n   - User Story: As a Merchant I want to view daily sales, conversion, and ROI metrics so that I can adjust strategies.\n   - Acceptance Criteria:\n     Given the last 30 days of data, when the dashboard loads, then it displays charts within 2 seconds and allows export to CSV.\n   - Data Inputs & Field Validations:\n     - Sale_Amount (decimal), Order_Count (int), Campaign_ID, Date.\n\n## 3. EXCEPTION FLOWS & EDGE CASES\n- Network & Connectivity Drops\n   - The system retries failed API calls up to 3 times with exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s). If still failing, queues the request in a durable queue for later.\n- Invalid Inputs & Concurrency Issues\n   - All inputs are validated server‑side; duplicate SKU updates are serialized via optimistic locking (version field). On conflict, return 409 Conflict.\n- System Recovery & Error Notifications\n   - Errors trigger alerts to DevOps via Slack & email; the system logs are stored in a central audit log.\n\n## 4. NON-FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS\n- Performance Metrics\n   - API response time <= 200 ms for 95% of requests.\n   - Pricing engine recomputes all SKUs within 5 minutes.\n- Security\n   - Data at rest encrypted with AES-256.\n   - Token based auth (JWT) with 24h expiry, refresh tokens 30 days.\n   - OWASP Top 10 controls: XSS, CSRF, Injection mitigated.\n- Scalability & Availability\n   - Auto‑scaling groups with min 2 nodes, max 10.\n   - 99.9% uptime SLA, graceful failover across 2 AZs.\n\n## 5. System Overview & Scope\n\n- **System Purpose**: AutoSellHub is a no‑code, AI‑driven automation platform that enables small e‑commerce shop owners to centrally manage product pricing, real‑time inventory, and multi‑channel advertising (Instagram, Facebook, Google Shopping) without writing code. The system reduces manual effort, improves price competitiveness, prevents stockouts, and accelerates ad campaign deployment to increase sales within days.\n\n- **Core Scope Boundaries**:  \n\n  **IN‑SCOPE**  \n  - User registration, authentication (JWT + MFA), password policy, role‑based access control per tenant.  \n  - No‑code UI builder for ad campaign creation (drag‑and‑drop, component library, validation).  \n  - AI pricing engine with configurable rules, competitor monitoring, demand elasticity.  \n  - Real‑time inventory synchronization across sales channels and internal catalog.  \n  - Multi‑channel ad scheduler with OAuth2 integration to Instagram, Facebook, Google Shopping APIs.  \n  - Multi‑tenant architecture with schema isolation, resource quotas, tenant‑specific RBAC.  \n  - CI/CD pipelines, Docker containerization, Kubernetes orchestration, automated testing.  \n  - Monitoring (Prometheus/Grafana), alerting, immutable audit logging, GDPR‑compliant data handling.  \n\n  **OUT‑OF‑SCOPE**  \n  - Full ERP or advanced CRM modules.  \n  - Third‑party logistics (shipping, fulfillment) integration.  \n  - B2B wholesale pricing or complex discount structures beyond rule‑based AI.  \n  - Manual order processing or inventory reconciliation beyond automated sync.  \n  - Custom code development beyond the no‑code builder.  \n\n## ⚙️ 2. Functional Requirements & Technical Specifications\n\n### 🔹 Module: User Management\n\n- **FR-1.0: User Registration & Initial Tenant Creation**  \n  - **Description**: New shop owners submit email, username, and password. System validates email uniqueness, enforces password policy, sends verification link, creates a tenant record and initial owner role.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Registration latency < 2\u202fs, password hash using Argon2id (64\u202fMiB memory, 3 iterations), email verification link expires after 48\u202fh, tenant creation < 5\u202fs.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Duplicate email → HTTP\u202f409 with “Email already registered”. Weak password → HTTP\u202f400 with list of policy violations. Email send failure → retry up to 3× with exponential backoff; after failure user must request resend.\n\n- **FR-1.1: Authentication with MFA**  \n  - **Description**: Users supply username/password then a time‑based one‑time password (TOTP). Valid credentials and MFA token produce a signed JWT (RS256) with scopes reflecting tenant roles.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Auth latency < 1\u202fs, MFA verification < 500\u202fms, JWT access token expiry 15\u202fmin, refresh token expiry 24\u202fh, token revocation stored in Redis for immediate invalidation.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Invalid credentials → HTTP\u202f401. MFA failure >3 attempts → account locked 15\u202fmin, notification sent. Revoked token → HTTP\u202f401 on subsequent request.\n\n- **FR-1.2: Password Policy & Management**  \n  - **Description**: Enforce minimum length 12 chars, upper/lower/digit/special, no reuse of last 5 passwords, expiration 90\u202fdays. Users can change password or request reset via email link.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Password hash Argon2id (64\u202fMiB, 3\u202fiter), password history 5 entries, change operation < 1\u202fs, reset email delivery < 5\u202fs.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Password reuse → HTTP\u202f400 with “Password already used”. Expired reset token → HTTP\u202f400 with “Link expired; request new one”.\n\n- **FR-1.3: Role‑Based Access Control (RBAC) per Tenant**  \n  - **Description**: Define roles Owner, Admin, Editor, Viewer. Permissions are attached to roles (e.g., Owner: full CRUD, Admin: manage users, Editor: manage products/pricing, Viewer: read‑only). Role assignments are stored in tenant‑scoped database.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Role assignment latency < 1\u202fs, permission check overhead < 5\u202fms per request, max 5 roles per tenant.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Unauthorized action → HTTP\u202f403 with audit log entry. Role assignment exceeding quota → HTTP\u202f409.\n\n### 🔹 Module: No‑Code UI Builder (Ad Scheduler)\n\n- **FR-2.0: Drag‑and‑Drop Campaign Builder**  \n  - **Description**: UI builder presents a component library (Image Upload, Text Overlay, Schedule Picker, Channel Selector). Users assemble components into a campaign canvas, validate inputs, and save.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Save operation < 2\u202fs, component library version control per tenant, max 10 components per campaign, UI state persistence via local storage.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Invalid image format (non‑JPEG/PNG) → HTTP\u202f400 with “Unsupported format”. Schedule conflict (overlapping time slots) → HTTP\u202f409 with conflict details. Save failure (DB error) → retry 3× with backoff; after failure user notified.\n\n- **FR-2.1: Multi‑Channel Integration (Instagram, Facebook, Google Shopping)**  \n  - **Description**: Builder exports campaign JSON which is pushed via secure HTTPS to each platform’s API using OAuth2 tokens stored encrypted. Scheduler triggers publishing at defined timestamps.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: API call latency < 200\u202fms per platform, rate limit 1\u202f000 calls/min per tenant, retry up to 3 attempts with exponential backoff, token refresh on 401.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: OAuth token expired → automatic refresh; platform API down → queue for retry; quota exceeded → HTTP\u202f429 with “Rate limit exceeded”.\n\n### 🔹 Module: AI Pricing Engine\n\n- **FR-3.0: Automated Price Adjustment**  \n  - **Description**: System fetches competitor prices and demand signals every 5\u202fmin, applies tenant‑defined pricing rules, calculates new price, and writes to product catalog.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Pricing calculation < 100\u202fms per SKU, update frequency 5\u202fmin, price delta capped at ±20\u202f% of current price, rule evaluation < 50\u202fms per cycle.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Competitor data unavailable → fallback to static rule; pricing rule validation failure → campaign paused, notification sent.\n\n- **FR-3.1: Pricing Rule Management**  \n  - **Description**: Tenants create/edit pricing rules (competitor monitoring, demand elasticity, discount thresholds). Rules are validated against schema before activation.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Rule creation < 2\u202fs, rule edit < 1\u202fs, rule evaluation < 50\u202fms, max 100 rules per tenant.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Invalid rule syntax → HTTP\u202f400 with validation errors. Rule exceeds tenant limit → HTTP\u202f409.\n\n### 🔹 Module: Real‑Time Inventory Sync\n\n- **FR-4.0: Inventory Synchronization Service**  \n  - **Description**: Pulls inventory counts from POS, ERP, and sales channels every 30\u202fs, reconciles with internal catalog, writes updates, and triggers alerts on anomalies.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Sync latency < 30\u202fs, update throughput 10\u202fk SKUs/min, inventory negative detection < 1\u202fs, database write latency < 50\u202fms.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Network timeout → retry up to 5×; inventory discrepancy → alert admin, freeze sales for affected SKU.\n\n- **FR-4.1: Manual Inventory Adjustment & Reconciliation**  \n  - **Description**: Allows bulk adjustments via CSV upload or UI, validates against current stock, generates reconciliation report.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Adjustment latency < 1\u202fs, CSV processing 5\u202fk rows < 2\u202fs, report generation < 5\u202fs.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Adjustment leads to negative stock → operation blocked, error logged.\n\n### 🔹 Module: Analytics & KPI Dashboard\n\n- **FR-5.0: Real‑Time Dashboard Rendering**  \n  - **Description**: Consolidated view of sales volume, pricing impact, ad performance, inventory health. Data refreshed every 60\u202fs, charts rendered client‑side.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Dashboard load < 3\u202fs, data refresh interval 60\u202fs, chart rendering < 500\u202fms, max 10 concurrent dashboard sessions per tenant.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Data source unreachable → stale data displayed with banner warning; user can trigger manual refresh.\n\n### 🔹 Module: Multi‑Tenancy & Data Isolation\n\n- **FR-6.0: Tenant Provisioning & Schema Isolation**  \n  - **Description**: Each shop becomes a tenant with separate PostgreSQL schema, Redis namespace, and storage bucket. Tenant creation includes resource quotas (CPU, memory, storage).  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Tenant creation < 5\u202fs, schema creation < 1\u202fs, query latency per tenant < 100\u202fms, storage quota 5\u202fGB per tenant.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Quota exceeded → HTTP\u202f403 with “Tenant resource limit reached”. Cross‑tenant query attempt → HTTP\u202f403.\n\n- **FR-6.1: Tenant‑Specific RBAC & Permission Propagation**  \n  - **Description**: Roles and permissions are scoped to tenant; changes propagate instantly via cache invalidation.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Permission check < 5\u202fms, role change propagation < 2\u202fs.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Permission escalation attempt → audit log, HTTP\u202f403.\n\n### 🔹 Module: CI/CD & Deployment Automation\n\n- **FR-7.0: Automated Build & Test Pipeline**  \n  - **Description**: Source code triggers GitHub Actions → Docker image build → unit/integration tests → security scans → artifact push to Artifact Registry.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Build time < 10\u202fmin, test coverage > 90\u202f%, security scan completion < 15\u202fmin, pipeline failure notification within 5\u202fmin.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Test failure → pipeline aborts, PR blocked; security vulnerability → automatic rollback to previous stable image.\n\n- **FR-7.1: Containerization & Kubernetes Orchestration**  \n  - **Description**: Services packaged in Docker images (< 500\u202fMB). Helm charts define deployments, services, ingress. Horizontal pod autoscaler scales based on CPU > 70\u202f% for 5\u202fmin.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Image size < 500\u202fMB, pod startup < 30\u202fs, auto‑scale min 2 pods, max 10 pods, rollout duration < 5\u202fmin, rollback < 2\u202fmin.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Pod health check failure → restart; if 3 consecutive failures → replace pod.\n\n- **FR-7.2: Monitoring, Alerting & Observability**  \n  - **Description**: Prometheus collects metrics (CPU, memory, request latency). Grafana dashboards visualize. Alertmanager sends alerts to Slack/PagerDuty. Immutable audit logs stored in Cloud Storage with AES‑256.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Metric collection latency < 1\u202fs, alert response time < 2\u202fmin, log retention 365\u202fdays, log write latency < 2\u202fs.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Alert threshold breach → notification; log write failure → retry 3×; after failure, critical actions logged locally.\n\n- **FR-7.3: Security Hardening & API Gateway**  \n  - **Description**: API Gateway (Envoy) enforces mutual TLS, JWT validation, rate limiting (1\u202f000 req/min per tenant), WAF rules. Secrets managed via Secret Manager with 30‑day rotation.  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Rate‑limit enforcement < 10\u202fms, secret rotation interval 30\u202fdays, vulnerability scan < 15\u202fmin.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Rate limit breach → HTTP\u202f429; invalid JWT → HTTP\u202f401; WAF block → HTTP\u202f403.\n\n### 🔹 Module: Audit & Compliance\n\n- **FR-8.0: Immutable Audit Logging**  \n  - **Description**: All critical actions (user creation, price rule changes, ad schedule updates, tenant provisioning) are logged to append‑only storage with SHA‑256 hash chain. Logs retained 365\u202fdays, encrypted at rest (AES‑256).  \n  - **Metrics/Thresholds**: Log write latency < 2\u202fs, log size per action < 5\u202fKB, hash verification on read < 100\u202fms.  \n  - **Exception Flow**: Log storage failure → local buffer with periodic sync; after recovery, replay logs.\n\n## 📊 Edge‑Case & State Lifecycle Mapping\n\nCore business entities and their strict state‑machine definitions:\n\n| Entity | States | Valid Transitions | Exception Handling |\n|--------|--------|-------------------|--------------------|\n| **Shop (Tenant)** | `Created` → `Active` → `Suspended` → `Deleted` | `Created` → `Active` (owner verification) <br> `Active` → `Suspended` (quota breach, unpaid) <br> `Suspended` → `Active` (remediation) <br> Any → `Deleted` (explicit delete) | `Suspended` → `Active` requires admin approval; transition logs audit entry. |\n| **Product** | `Draft` → `Active` → `Discontinued` → `Archived` | `Draft` → `Active` (approval) <br> `Active` → `Discontinued` (manual) <br> `Discontinued` → `Archived` (30\u202fd inactivity) <br> `Active` → `Draft` (edit) | Invalid price range → block transition to `Active`, return error. |\n| **Inventory** | `InSync` → `OutOfSync` → `Updating` → `Error` | `InSync` → `Updating` (sync start) <br> `Updating` → `InSync` (success) <br> `Updating` → `OutOfSync` (partial failure) <br> `OutOfSync` → `Error` (manual intervention) | `Error` → `InSync` after admin correction; logs generated. |\n| **PricingRule** | `Defined` → `Active` → `Paused` → `Expired` | `Defined` → `Active` (validation pass) <br> `Active` → `Paused` (admin) <br> `Active` → `Expired` (time‑based) <br> `Paused` → `Active` (resume) | Validation failure → rule stays `Defined`, notification sent. |\n| **AdCampaign** | `Draft` → `Scheduled` → `Running` → `Completed` → `Cancelled` | `Draft` → `Scheduled` (schedule time) <br> `Scheduled` → `Running` (start) <br> `Running` → `Completed` (end) <br> Any → `Cancelled` (manual) | Platform API failure during `Running` → transition to `Error` (new state) and retry. |\n| **UserAccount** | `Invited` → `Active` → `Suspended` → `Deactivated` | `Invited` → `Active` (email verification) <br> `Active` → `Suspended` (MFA failure >5) <br> `Suspended` → `Active` (remediation) <br> Any → `Deactivated` (delete) | `Suspended` → `Active` requires password reset and re‑MFA. |\n| **Session** | `Valid` → `Expired` → `Revoked` | `Valid` → `Expired` (idle >15\u202fmin) <br> `Valid` → `Revoked` (logout / admin) | `Revoked` session token rejected with HTTP\u202f401. |\n\nAll transitions are logged in the audit trail with timestamps and user IDs.\n\n## 🛡️ 3. Non‑Functional Requirements (NFR)\n\n- **NFR‑1: Performance & Scalability**  \n  - **Throughput**: API must sustain 10\u202f000 requests per second (RPS) with response time ≤\u202f200\u202fms (95th percentile).  \n  - **Concurrent Users**: Support up to 5\u202f000 concurrent tenant users.  \n  - **Database**: PostgreSQL read latency ≤\u202f50\u202fms, write latency ≤\u202f100\u202fms.  \n  - **Auto‑Scaling**: Horizontal pod autoscaler scales from 2 to 10 instances based on CPU >\u202f70\u202f% for 5\u202fmin; database read replicas scale to 3.  \n  - **Availability**: 99.99\u202f% SLA (annual downtime ≤\u202f52.56\u202fminutes).  \n\n- **NFR‑2: Security & Compliance**  \n  - **Authentication**: JWT (RS256) with 15‑min access token, 24‑h refresh token, mandatory TOTP MFA.  \n  - **Authorization**: Role‑based access control per tenant, OAuth2 scopes, API Gateway rate limiting 1\u202f000 req/min per tenant.  \n  - **Password Policy**: Minimum 12 characters, upper/lower/digit/special, history 5, Argon2id hashing (64\u202fMiB, 3\u202fiter).  \n  - **Encryption**: TLS\u202f1.3 for all in‑transit traffic, AES‑256 at rest, secret rotation every 30\u202fdays.  \n  - **Audit & Logging**: Append‑only immutable logs, SHA‑256 hash chain, retention 365\u202fdays, GDPR‑compliant data handling.  \n  - **Vulnerability Management**: Weekly OWASP ZAP scans, automated remediation within 48\u202fh.  \n\n- **NFR‑3: Reliability & Availability**  \n  - **Uptime**: 99.99\u202f% monthly availability, RTO (Recovery Time Objective) ≤\u202f30\u202fminutes, RPO (Recovery Point Objective) ≤\u202f5\u202fminutes.  \n  - **Failover**: Multi‑AZ deployment across 2 Google Cloud regions, automatic fail‑over on pod or database node loss.  \n  - **Backup**: Daily full backups, point‑in‑time recovery to last 5\u202fminutes, stored in cross‑region bucket.  \n  - **Disaster Recovery**: Quarterly DR drills, documented runbooks, automated failover scripts.  \n\n## ⛓️ 4. Traceability & System Constraints\n\n- **Business Constraints**  \n  - **Budget**: Cap‑ex limited to $2\u202fM for first 12\u202fmonths; OPEX capped at $300\u202fk/year.  \n  - **Timeline**: MVP delivery within 6\u202fmonths, full feature set within 12\u202fmonths.  \n  - **Regulatory**: Must comply with GDPR, CCPA, and local data residency for EU customers (data stored in West‑Europe).  \n\n- **Technical Constraints**  \n  - **Platform**: Google Cloud Platform (GCP) with GKE for orchestration, Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL) for data store, Cloud Storage for objects, Secret Manager for secrets.  \n  - **Language/Runtime**: Node.js 20.x for backend services, Python 3.11 for AI pricing micro‑service, React 18 for admin UI.  \n  - **Security Standards**: OWASP Top\u202f10 mitigation, ISO\u202f27001 alignment, mandatory MFA, password hashing Argon2id.  \n  - **Integration**: OAuth2 with Instagram, Facebook, Google Shopping APIs; webhook support for inventory push.  \n\n- Phase 1: User Management, Authentication, and Authorization\n- **Original Objective**: In this phase, the coder sub-agent will implement user management, authentication, and authorization features. The tester sub-agent will write unit tests and integration tests for these features. The reviewer sub-agent will perform static code analysis and review the code for security compliance.\n- **Gap Analysis**: Original phase lacks MFA, password policy enforcement, role‑based access control per tenant, token revocation, audit logging, tenant provisioning, and secure session handling.\n- **Augmented Architecture & Solutions**:\n  - *Data Models/Schema*:\n    - `users`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `username`, `email`, `passwordHash`, `salt`, `status` (`Invited|Active|Suspended|Deactivated`), `mfaSecret`, `mfaEnabled`, `roles` (array of roleIds), `passwordHistory`, `passwordExpires`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `lastLogin`.\n    - `tenants`: `_id`, `name`, `domain`, `status` (`Created|Active|Suspended|Deleted`), `quota`, `ownerId`, `createdAt`.\n    - `roles`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `name`, `permissions`, `createdAt`.\n    - `permissions`: `_id`, `code`, `resource`, `action`.\n    - `sessions`: `_id`, `userId`, `jwtId`, `expiresAt`, `revoked`, `createdAt`.\n    - `auditLogs`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `userId`, `action`, `resource`, `details`, `timestamp`, `hash`.\n  - *State Machine Lifecycle*:\n    - **UserAccount**: `Invited → Active (email verification) → Suspended (MFA failure >5) → Deactivated (delete)`.\n    - **Tenant**: `Created → Active (owner verification) → Suspended (quota breach) → Deleted (explicit)`.\n  - *API & Event Contracts*:\n    - `POST /api/v1/auth/register` – payload `{username,email,password}` → `{userId,tenantId,status}`.\n    - `POST /api/v1/auth/verify-email` – payload `{token}`.\n    - `POST /api/v1/auth/login` – payload `{username,password,totpToken}` → `{accessToken,refreshToken,expiresIn}`.\n    - `POST /api/v1/auth/logout` – payload `{refreshToken}` revokes session.\n    - `GET /api/v1/users` – list users (RBAC filtered).\n    - **Events** (RabbitMQ direct exchange `user_events`):\n      - `user.events.created` → `user.created.queue`.\n      - `user.events.verified` → `user.verified.queue`.\n      - `user.events.login` → `user.login.queue`.\n      - `user.events.logout` → `user.logout.queue`.\n      - `user.events.suspended` → `user.suspended.queue`.\n      - `user.events.deactivated` → `user.deactivated.queue`.\n  - *Messaging Topology*:\n    - **Exchange**: `user_events` (direct).\n    - **Queues**: `user.created.queue`, `user.verified.queue`, `user.login.queue`, `user.logout.queue`, `user.suspended.queue`, `user.deactivated.queue`.\n    - **DLX**: `user_events.dlx` with `user_events.dlq`.\n    - **Routing Keys**: as above.\n    - **DLQ Failover**: Messages failing after 3 NACKs moved to DLQ; DLQ consumer logs alert to Slack/PagerDuty.\n\n### 5. Phase 2: Autonomous Pricing Engine, Inventory Sync, and Advertising Scheduler\n- **Original Objective**: In this phase, the coder sub-agent will develop the autonomous pricing engine, inventory sync, and advertising scheduler features. The tester sub-agent will write unit tests and integration tests for these features. The reviewer sub-agent will perform static code analysis and review the code for security compliance.\n- **Gap Analysis**: Original phase does not detail AI rule engine, competitor price fetching, demand elasticity, real‑time inventory CDC, multi‑channel OAuth integration, scheduling engine, state machines, alerting, or asynchronous messaging.\n- **Augmented Architecture & Solutions**:\n  - *Data Models/Schema*:\n    - `pricingRules`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `name`, `conditions`, `actions`, `status` (`Defined|Active|Paused|Expired`), `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `lastExecuted`.\n    - `products`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `sku`, `name`, `basePrice`, `cost`, `inventoryQty`, `pricingRuleId`, `status` (`Draft|Active|Discontinued|Archived`).\n    - `pricingHistory`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `productId`, `price`, `effectiveAt`, `source`, `reason`.\n    - `inventory`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `sku`, `quantity`, `lastUpdated`, `source`, `status` (`InSync|OutOfSync|Updating|Error`).\n    - `inventoryHistory`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `sku`, `quantity`, `changeType`, `timestamp`, `userId`.\n    - `adCampaigns`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `name`, `components` (array), `schedule` (`start`,`end`), `status` (`Draft|Scheduled|Running|Completed|Cancelled`), `channels`, `oauthTokens`, `createdAt`.\n    - `adComponents`: `_id`, `type`, `payload`, `position`, `campaignId`.\n    - `channelCredentials`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `platform`, `accessToken`, `refreshToken`, `tokenExpiry`, `scope`.\n  - *State Machine Lifecycle*:\n    - **PricingRule**: `Defined → Active (validation) → Paused (admin) → Expired (time) → (re‑enable to Defined)`.\n    - **Inventory**: `InSync → Updating (sync start) → InSync (success) / OutOfSync (partial) → Error (failure) → InSync after correction`.\n    - **AdCampaign**: `Draft → Scheduled (schedule time) → Running (publishing) → Completed (end) / Cancelled (manual) → (re‑enter Draft if edited)`.\n  - *API & Event Contracts*:\n    - `POST /api/v1/pricing/rules` – create rule.\n    - `PUT /api/v1/pricing/rules/{id}` – update.\n    - `GET /api/v1/pricing/rules` – list.\n    - `POST /api/v1/pricing/evaluate` – payload `{productId}` → `{newPrice,reason}`.\n    - `POST /api/v1/inventory/sync` – payload `{source,skus}` → `{status,updatedCount}`.\n    - `POST /api/v1/inventory/adjust` – bulk adjust via payload.\n    - `POST /api/v1/ads/campaigns` – create campaign.\n    - `PUT /api/v1/ads/campaigns/{id}/schedule` – set schedule.\n    - `POST /api/v1/ads/publish` – payload `{campaignId}`.\n    - **Events** (exchange `business_events` direct):\n      - `pricing.rules.created` → `pricing.rules.queue`.\n      - `pricing.rules.updated` → `pricing.rules.queue`.\n      - `pricing.calculated` → `pricing.calculated.queue`.\n      - `inventory.synced` → `inventory.sync.queue`.\n      - `inventory.adjusted` → `inventory.adjusted.queue`.\n      - `ads.campaign.created` → `ads.campaign.queue`.\n      - `ads.campaign.scheduled` → `ads.campaign.queue`.\n      - `ads.published` → `ads.publish.queue`.\n  - *Messaging Topology*:\n    - **Exchange**: `business_events` (direct).\n    - **Queues**: `pricing.rules.queue`, `pricing.calculated.queue`, `inventory.sync.queue`, `inventory.adjusted.queue`, `ads.campaign.queue`, `ads.publish.queue`.\n    - **DLX**: `business_events.dlx` with `business_events.dlq`.\n    - **Routing Keys**: as above.\n    - **DLQ Failover**: 3‑retry limit, then DLQ; DLQ consumer triggers admin alert.\n\n### 6. Phase 3: Analytics Dashboard, KPI Reporting, and Data Visualization\n- **Original Objective**: In this phase, the coder sub-agent will build the analytics dashboard, KPI reporting, and data visualization features. The tester sub-agent will write unit tests and integration tests for these features. The reviewer sub-agent will perform static code analysis and review the code for security compliance.\n- **Gap Analysis**: Original phase lacks real‑time metric pipeline, role‑based data access, dashboard configuration storage, visualization definitions, alerting thresholds, and asynchronous event handling.\n- **Augmented Architecture & Solutions**:\n  - *Data Models/Schema*:\n    - `dashboardConfigs`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `userId`, `widgets` (array), `refreshInterval`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`.\n    - `widgets`: `_id`, `type`, `title`, `query`, `options`, `position`.\n    - `kpiMetrics`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `metricName`, `value`, `timestamp`, `source`.\n    - `reports`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `name`, `definition`, `schedule`, `lastRun`, `status`.\n    - `alertRules`: `_id`, `tenantId`, `metric`, `threshold`, `severity`, `notificationChannels`, `enabled`.\n  - *State Machine Lifecycle*:\n    - **Dashboard**: `Enabled → Updating (refresh) → Refreshed (data loaded) → Stale (if older than interval) → Enabled`.\n    - **KPI Metric**: `Computed → Stored → Validated → Alerted (if threshold breach)`.\n  - *API & Event Contracts*:\n    - `GET /api/v1/analytics/dashboard` – returns config.\n    - `PUT /api/v1/analytics/dashboard` – update config.\n    - `GET /api/v1/analytics/metrics` – query metrics; payload `{start,end,metricNames}`.\n    - `POST /api/v1/analytics/reports` – create report.\n    - `GET /api/v1/analytics/reports/{id}/run` – trigger execution.\n    - `POST /api/v1/analytics/alerts` – acknowledge alert.\n    - **Events** (exchange `analytics_events` direct):\n      - `metric.computed` → `metrics.queue`.\n      - `dashboard.refreshed` → `dashboard.queue`.\n      - `report.completed` → `reports.queue`.\n      - `alert.triggered` → `alerts.queue`.\n  - *Messaging Topology*:\n    - **Exchange**: `analytics_events` (direct).\n    - **Queues**: `metrics.queue`, `dashboard.queue`, `reports.queue`, `alerts.queue`.\n    - **DLX**: `analytics_events.dlx` with `analytics_events.dlq`.\n    - **Routing Keys**: as above.\n    - **DLQ Failover**: 2‑retry limit, then DLQ; DLQ consumer sends Slack/PagerDuty notification.\n\n### 7. Phase 4: Multi-Tenancy, Data Isolation, and Security Features\n- **Original Objective**: In this phase, the coder sub-agent will implement multi-tenancy, data isolation, and security features. payload `{name,ownerEmail}`.\n    - `GET /api/v1/tenants/{id}` – retrieve tenant.\n    - `PUT /api/v1/tenants/{id}/quota` – update quotas.\n    - `GET /api/v1/security/policies` – list policies.\n    - `POST /api/v1/security/policies` – create policy.\n    - `GET /api/v1/audit/logs` – query audit logs (tenant filtered).\n    - **Events** (exchange `tenant_events` direct):\n      - `tenant.provisioned` → `tenant.provisioned.queue`.\n      - `tenant.suspended` → `tenant.suspended.queue`.\n      - `tenant.deleted` → `tenant.deleted.queue`.\n      - `security.policy.created` → `security.policy.queue`.\n  - *Messaging Topology*:\n    - **Exchange**: `tenant_events` (direct).\n    - **Queues**: `tenant.provisioned.queue`, `tenant.suspended.queue`, `tenant.deleted.queue`, `security.policy.queue`.\n    - **DLX**: `tenant_events.dlx` with `tenant_events.dlq`.\n    - **Routing Keys**: as above.\n    - **DLQ Failover**: 3‑retry limit, then DLX; DLX triggers admin alert.\n\n### 8. Phase 5: Cross-System Integration, Performance Profiling, and Production Deployment\n- **Original Objective**: In this phase, the coder sub-agent will perform cross-system integration and ensure that all components work together seamlessly. The GKE sub-agent will configure Kubernetes clusters and deploy the application to production.\n- **Gap Analysis**: Original phase lacks CI/CD pipeline definition, container image specifications, Helm chart structure, monitoring & alerting stack, disaster recovery, rollback procedures, performance benchmarks, load testing, and security hardening (API gateway, mutual TLS, WAF). Also missing observability configuration.\n- **Augmented Architecture & Solutions**:\n  - *Data Models/Schema*:\n    - `deployments`: `_id`, `appVersion`, `image`, `namespace`, `replicas`, `status` (`Pending|InProgress|Completed|RolledBack`), `createdAt`, `completedAt`, `triggeredBy`.\n    - `rollbacks`: `_id`, `deploymentId`, `revision`, `reason`, `initiatedBy`, `timestamp`.\n    - `monitoringMetrics`: `_id`, `metricName`, `value`, `timestamp`, `source`.\n    - `alerts`: `_id`, `severity`, `message`, `source`, `timestamp`, `acknowledged`, `resolved`.\n    - `performanceProfiles`: `_id`, `testName`, `targetRPS`, `p95Latency`, `successRate`, `results`, `executedAt`.\n  - *State Machine Lifecycle*:\n    - **Deployment**: `Planned → InProgress → Completed → RolledBack`.\n    - **Alert**: `Triggered → Acknowledged → Resolved`.\n  - *API & Event Contracts*:\n    - `POST /api/v1/deployments` – trigger deployment; payload `{image,replicas}`.\n    - `GET /api/v1/deployments` – list deployments.\n    - `POST /api/v1/deployments/{id}/rollback` – rollback.\n    - `GET /api/v1/monitoring/metrics` – query metrics.\n    - `POST /api/v1/alerts` – acknowledge alert.\n    - **Events** (exchange `integration_events` direct):\n      - `deployment.triggered` → `deployment.queue`.\n      - `deployment.completed` → `deployment.queue`.\n      - `deployment.rolledback` → `deployment.queue`.\n      - `metric.collected` → `monitoring.queue`.\n      - `alert.triggered` → `alert.queue`.\n  - *Messaging Topology*:\n    - **Exchange**: `integration_events` (direct).\n    - **Queues**: `deployment.queue`, `monitoring.queue`, `alert.queue`.\n    - **DLX**: `integration_events.dlx` with `integration_events.dlq`.\n    - **Routing Keys**: as above.\n    - **DLQ Failover**: 3‑retry limit, then DLQ; DLQ consumer sends PagerDuty alert.\n\n## 🛡️ 3. Enterprise Compliance, High Availability & Disaster Recovery\n- **Security Boundaries**:\n  - **API Gateway (Envoy)** enforces mutual TLS, JWT validation, tenant‑specific rate limiting (1\u202f000 req/min), and WAF rules (OWASP Top\u202f10). All internal service‑to‑service calls use mTLS.\n  - **Authentication**: JWT (RS256) with 15‑min access token, 24‑h refresh token, mandatory TOTP MFA, Argon2id password hashing (64\u202fMiB, 3\u202fiter), password history of 5, expiration 90\u202fdays.\n  - **Authorization**: Role‑Based Access Control per tenant, scopes attached to JWT, permission cache in Redis (TTL 30\u202fs).\n  - **Encryption**: TLS\u202f1.3 for all inbound/outbound traffic; MongoDB encrypted at rest (AES‑256); secrets stored in Secret Manager with 30‑day rotation.\n  - **Audit & Logging**: Immutable append‑only logs stored in Cloud Storage with SHA‑256 hash chain; log retention 365\u202fdays; log write latency <\u202f2\u202fs; audit events for all critical actions (user creation, price rule changes, ad schedule updates, tenant provisioning).\n  - **Vulnerability Management**: Weekly OWASP ZAP scans, automated remediation within 48\u202fh, secret scanning in CI pipeline.\n\n- **Asynchronous Failover Logic**:\n  - All message queues (RabbitMQ) are clustered across multiple zones. Each queue has a **Dead‑Letter Exchange (DLX)** with a dedicated DLQ (e.g., `user_events.dlq`).\n  - Consumer logic: on NACK, increment retry count stored in Redis; if retries ≤ max (3 for business events, 2 for analytics), requeue; otherwise move to DLQ via `x-dead-letter-exchange`.\n  - DLQ consumer processes: logs failure, increments alert counter, and pushes notification to Slack/PagerDuty (configurable channels).\n  - Auto‑scaling of consumer pods based on queue depth (target 70\u202f% of max depth) using Horizontal Pod Autoscaler.\n\n- **High Availability & Disaster Recovery**:\n  - **Multi‑AZ Deployment**: Services deployed on GKE across two Google Cloud regions (e.g., us‑central1‑a, europe‑west1‑b). StatefulSets for MongoDB with three replicas, each in separate zones.\n  - **Failover**: Kubernetes Cluster Autoscaler and Regional Load Balancers provide automatic pod and IP failover. RabbitMQ clustering with mirrored queues ensures message durability.\n  - **Backup & Recovery**: Daily full backups of MongoDB to Cloud Storage with point‑in‑time recovery up to 5\u202fminutes (using Google Cloud Backup for Cloud SQL). RTO ≤\u202f30\u202fminutes, RPO ≤\u202f5\u202fminutes.\n  - **Disaster Recovery Drills**: Quarterly DR exercises with documented runbooks, automated failover scripts, and communication plans. All critical configurations stored in Terraform state locked in Cloud Source Repository.\n  - **Monitoring & Alerting**: Prometheus collects metrics (CPU, memory, request latency, queue depth). Grafana dashboards visualize SLA compliance. Alertmanager routes alerts to Slack (channel #incidents) and PagerDuty (critical severity). Alert thresholds: CPU >\u202f70\u202f% for 5\u202fmin, latency p95 >\u202f200\u202fms, queue depth >\u202f1000.\n\nThis blueprint preserves all original phases, augments them with concrete data schemas, state‑machine lifecycles, API contracts, and a robust asynchronous messaging topology with DLQ failover, and aligns the architecture with the detailed SRS requirements while maintaining the original technology stack and timeline constraints.', pydantic=None, json_dict=None, agent='Enterprise System Architect', output_format=<OutputFormat.RAW: 'raw'>, messages=[{'role': 'system', 'content': 'You are Enterprise System Architect. # 📐 Agent Specification: Enterprise System Architect (SA)\n\n## 📌 1.
